[Table_Title] 
假期扰动 PMI走势，内外需双双放缓

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月PMI数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月02日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件： 10月中采制造业PMI为 49.3%，较上月下跌0.8个
百分点，非制造业PMI由50.2%下跌至49.7%。 
⚫ 制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气跌幅较大。PMI
再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响。中、小
型企业PMI回落幅度较大，均处于临近值下方，大型企业
PMI小幅回落，仍处于景气区间。 
⚫ 产需双双回落，进出口景气度降温，价格指标景气度回落。
生产指数降至临界值下方，新订单指数下跌，生产回落快于
需求。新出口订单、进口指数下跌，而欧美日等制造业PMI
景气度整体保持旺盛。企业采购意愿下滑，生产放缓影响企
业采购需求。纺织、有色金属、非金属矿等行业生产、新订
单偏弱。原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企
业成本压力边际缓解，有色金属板块价格跌幅较大，能耗产
业链价格维持高景气。原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的
趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。 
⚫ 高技术、装备制造高位放缓，消费品、基础原材料、钢铁行
业景气度下滑。新经济景气度高位回落，但仍处景气区间，
EPMI大幅拉升，供需持续好转，秋季旺季特征明显，采购
价格持续上行，但速度放缓。SMI中枢上行，中上游制造业
显著修复。消费品行业PMI重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉动
消退后，内需接续效应不足。兰格钢铁PMI下滑幅度较大，
销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，供需均转弱。 
⚫ 建筑业为非制造业PMI主要拖累，服务业PMI处于临界点。
非制造业PMI新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，需求
持续偏弱，价格指标回落。其中，建筑业PMI下滑至临界值
下方，工作日减少带来一定影响，新订单指数跌幅较大，建
筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一
定拖累。服务业PMI小幅放缓，新订单指数跌幅较大，价格
指数回落，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。 
⚫ 季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支
撑。10月受长假和外部扰动等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需
均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售前瞻
指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定
压力，融资环境指数小幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有
望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资好转。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及
预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积 
财政支出有望继续提速

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年10月，中采制造业PMI为49.3%， 较上月下跌0.8个百分点， 非制造业PMI为49.7%，
较上月下跌0.5个百分点，综合PMI为49.7%，环比下跌1个百分点。

1    PMI环比季节性回落，产需双双放缓 
制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气回落幅度较大。制造业PMI环比回落0.8个
百分点至49.3%，再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响，财政投放提速，
项目建设给PMI表现形成支撑。从不同规模企业来看，小型企业PMI下行1.4个百分点
至47.5%， 中型企业PMI下行1.3个百分点至48.4%， 大型企业PMI由50.6%回落至50.2%，
仅大型企业处于景气区间，中小企业依然承压。 
高技术、装备制造高位继续回落，战略新兴产业显著回暖，消费品、基础原材料、钢
铁行业景气度下滑。高技术制造业继续下行1.2个百分点至51.3%，装备制造业由51%
回落为50.6%，高端制造业景气度持续回落，但仍处景气区间。基础原材料行业由48%
回落为47.3%。消费品行业下行1.9个百分点至48.8%，重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉
动消退后，内需接续效应不足。战略性新兴产业PMI（EPMI）由52.3%拉升至58%，达
到年内高点，其中，生产量、产品订货和进口指数上行幅度较大，供需双双好转，秋
季旺季特征明显， 但进口指数仍处荣枯线下方， 采购价格持续上行， 但速度明显放缓，
就业和研发指标也有所上行。光伏经理人指数（SMI）10月中枢较9月上行，中上游制
造业显著修复，下游电站小幅回落。兰格钢铁PMI由49.8%回落为46.9%，下滑幅度较
大，其中，销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，下行至荣枯线下方，销量、总订单、国内
订单也明显回落，出口订单量小幅下滑，仅库存水平环比上行。

图表1：10月制造业PMI环比季节性回落（%） 图表2：高技术制造、装备制造业PMI继续回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

口指数环比转为下跌2.2个百分点至47%。采购量下行2.1个百分点至48.9%，生产放缓
影响企业采购需求回落。供货商配送时间由50.1%上行至50.2%。从统计局公布数据来
看，铁路船舶航空航天设备、计算机通信电子设备等行业生产指数和新订单指数均位
于53.0%及以上；纺织、非金属矿物制品、有色金属冶炼及压延加工等行业生产指数
和新订单指数均低于临界点，地产持续偏弱对上游的拖累作用持续，部分有色金属价
格、库存偏高给产需带来一定影响。美国制造业PMI高位边际放缓，但景气度仍强，
日本制造业PMI持续小幅回落，仍处荣枯线上方。德国、英国制造业PMI上行，处于临
界点上方，法国受国内动乱影响，制造业PMI降至荣枯线下方。 
图表3：供需分项环比回落，价格指标下行（%） 图表4：美国制造业PMI小幅回落，景气仍强（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 
价格指标景气度回落，原材料和加工行业剪刀差收窄，有色行业价格下滑为主要拖
累，能化产业链维持高景气。主要原材料购进价格回落4个百分点至56.8%，出厂价格
下行2.3个百分点至51.7%，原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企业成本
压力边际缓解。10月，受到宏观流动性收紧预期和基本面供需矛盾等因素影响，有色
金属板块承压，金、银、钴、碳酸锂等有色金属跌幅较大。国际油价持续攀升，石油
煤炭及其他燃料加工、化学纤维及橡胶塑料制品等行业价格指数维持高景气。 
原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。产成品库
存由47.6%下跌至47.5%， 原材料库存上行0.2个百分点至48.4%。原料端价格压力持续，
企业备库需求和价格增长带动原材料库存上行。生产经营活动预期指标由53.8%回落
为53.4%。 
图表5：原材料库存累积，产成品库存去化（%） 图表6：原材料价格和出厂价格价差明显收窄（%）

2    非制造业PMI回落，建筑业为主要拖累 
非制造业PMI回落，需求持续偏弱，价格指标回落。10月非制造业PMI为49.7%，较上
月下跌0.5个百分点。其中，新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，持续处于临近
值下方，在手订单指数也有所回落。商务活动指数下滑，降至临近值下方。投入品价
格回落幅度大于销售价格回落幅度。业务活动预期由55.5%回落至54.7%，企业经营预
期下跌，但仍处景气区间。仅从业人员由45.9%小幅上行至46%。 
建筑业PMI下滑至临界值下方，新订单指数下滑幅度较大，工作日减少带来一定影响，
销售价格和投入品价格走势分化。建筑业PMI下跌至48%，上月为50.3%。其中，建筑
业新订单指数下跌5.7个百分点至40%， 建筑业业务活动预期下跌3.1个百分点至52.3%，
建筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一定拖累。随着财政资金
落地， “六张网”建设逐步显效，有望带动建筑业回暖。 
服务业PMI处于临界点，新订单指数持续偏弱，价格指数回落。服务业PMI为50%，较
上月下跌0.2个百分点。服务业新订单指数下跌，回落幅度相对最大。服务业投入品
和销售价格均下跌，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。业务活动预期指数小
幅回落，仍处临界值上方。从统计局公布数据来看，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、
货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数持续位于55.0%以上的高景气区间；资本市
场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数低位运行。

图表7：建筑业PMI新订单跌幅较大（%） 图表8：服务业新订单下跌(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

制造业用工需求持平，服务业就业好转，建筑业就业回落。10月制造业PMI从业人员
持平于上月的48.4%。服务业从业人员较上月上行0.5个百分点至46.7%，建筑业从业
人员指数为42%，较上月下跌2.3个百分点，十一长假影响建筑业用工需求，给服务业
就业带来提振。

3    总结与展望 
季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支撑。10月受长假和外部扰动
等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售
前瞻指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定压力，融资环境指数小
幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资
好转。

图表9：企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落（%） 图表10：企业融资环境指数小幅上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

4    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。

### Page 2

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善
1

 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡

― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算

### Page 12

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

12

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

―7～9月期は、純輸出が＋1.8％Ptと成長をけん引する一方、総資本形成は＋0.6％Ptまで縮小。内需の弱さ鮮明

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

16
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

米国債券市場：インフレ加速懸念で上昇後、AI関連株の下落を受け金利は低下
 10月の米10年国債利回りは、一時5.3％台まで上昇したものの、足元では5.0％前後まで低下
― 月初は、原油高等によるインフレ加速懸念を受け、長期金利は約24年ぶりの高水準となる5.3％台まで上昇
― 中旬以降は、AI関連株の先行きに対する慎重な見方から、安全資産である米国債への需要が拡大し、長期金利は低下
 今後も、中東情勢に伴う原油価格の変動やAI関連株の動向など、金利を左右する不確実性が多く、振れの大きい展開が続
く可能性

― 財政悪化が懸念される一部欧州諸国の国債から資金が流出する一方、独国債への資金流入が拡大
― なお根強い利上げ期待を背景に、イールドカーブはブルフラット化
 ECB高官からは「慎重な対応」が妥当との発言。市場は引き続き追加利上げ実施を予想
― ラガルド総裁は、9月理事会以降の長期金利上昇によってインフレ圧力が抑制される可能性を示唆。市場の利上げ織り込

みは幾分後退も、追加利上げへの期待は残存

（％）
国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

内外株式：AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落
 日米株は一時上昇も、米新興クラウドサービスに対する信用不安などからAI・半導体関連株を中心に下落
― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に。もっとも、S&P500はヘルスケア関連株等への資金シフトが進み、底堅く推移
 AIインフラ投資に対する信用不安は残存。原油価格の上昇も重石となり、日米株は軟調な推移が続くと予想
― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

Document 024: 2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略より21 世紀ニッポンの「 ア
ポロ計画」を 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 202 6 年 10 月 27 日

心情的にはノーサレンダー路線 
先月の論考では、①度重なる成長戦略にもかかわらず日本の潜在成長率は低下したこと、②成長戦略
には政策手段の特定が困難という基本問題があるこ と 、③25 年英知を尽くしても結果が出なかった事実
は厳粛に受け止めるべきこと、などを述べた 1。 
それを踏まえ、本稿ではあるべき方向性を考えてみたい。選択肢は、(a)25 年で諦めず50 年でも100
年でもやる、(b)成長目標を達成しやすい水準に下げる、(c)政府のミッションは成長戦略とは別にある
と考える、の 3 つである。選択肢(b)が無難に見えるが、もともと成長戦略は「現状の成長率ではだめ
だ」という認識に基づくものである。現状を明確に上回る成長率を目指さない限り、成長戦略としては
自己矛盾である。 
すると残るのは (a)ノーサレンダー路線、(c)ミッション再考、 のいずれかである。このうちノーサレ
ンダー路線、つまり「成長を諦めない」という不屈の姿勢には人々の共感が得られやすい。しかし、不
屈の志を掲げても、手段の裏付けを欠くという現実は変わらない。手段の裏付けを欠くなら「目標」で
はなく「夢」である。もちろん、夢を語ることが悪いわけではないし、政治家は夢を語るべきという考
えもあるだろう。その夢が偶然にでもかなえば歴史に残る偉業であり、夢なら実現せずとも落胆の度合
いは小さい。ノーサレンダー路線はアップサイドだけでダウンサイドはない、と考えてよいだろうか。 
成長戦略のダウンサイド 
成長戦略にダウンサイドはない、やってみて損はない、と い う前提は実は正しくない。成長戦略は「た
だ」でできるわけではないからだ。例えば、成長戦略には多かれ少なかれネット財政支出が伴う。国債
をどれだけ発行できるのか議論の余地はあるが、いずれにせよ有限である。 
一方、成長戦略は総花的になりやすい。成長を実現する手段がはっきりしない以上、ある政策案を「成
長とは無関係」ときっぱり排除するのも難しいからである。経済界の要望や政治家の信念に基づく様々
な政策案が、成長に少しでも関係しそうなら幅広く正当化される。各種施策の費用対効果について一定

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「成長戦略の本質的な限界」 （2026年 9 月25 日 ）。

### Page 2

2

の分析や議論は行われている。しかし、行われることとそれが有効に機能することは別である。費用対
効果の分析が有効に機能し、各種施策の経済成長へのインパクトが正確に測れていたとしたら、25 年に
わたり成長目標の大幅未達という結果にはならなかったはずである。 
一般に、政策目標の実現に向けてリソースが投入され、目標が実現できなかった場合、そのリソース
投入は失敗だったことになる。チャレンジ自体は無駄ではなかったと言える場合もあるが、過去 25 年
だめだった目標にもう一度挑戦してみるという話なら、たぶん次もだめだろうと事前に予想できる。金
も人もふんだんにあるなら別だが、財政状況が厳しく、人材の不足も叫ばれる国で、高確率で失敗する
ミッションへのリソース投入は賢明な選択と言えるのか。 
有限のリソースが無駄に使われれば、他の課題のために使えるリソースが減る。ここで言うリソース
は財政資金だけではない。実現できない目的のために、多忙な官僚の労力が割かれ、政治家のポリティ
カル ・ キャピタルも消耗すれば、やればできたはずの他の課題への対応まで中途半端に終わってしまう。
成長戦略には他の課題を犠牲にするという「機会費用」が伴うのであり、それが「ただではない」とい
うことのより正確な意味である。「成長を諦めないノーサレンダー路線でいく」という選択は、「50 年で
も 100 年でもその機会費用を払い続けることを覚悟する」という選択でもある。 
何のための経済成長か 
ここで話を一段階前に戻してみよう。そもそもなぜ、経済成長を高めるべきと考えられているのだろ
うか。もちろん、低成長より高成長の方がいいに決まっているので、経済成長を簡単に高められるので
あれば、そんな疑問を持つ必要はない。しかし、経済成長を高めようとすることで機会費用を払い続け
ることになる確率が高いなら、そうまでしてわれわれが最終的に望んでいるものは何なのかという問い
に、きちんと向き合う必要がある。 
経済成長の必要性を訴える主張は、「〇〇の解決には経済成長が不可欠」「低成長のままなら〇〇のよ
うな不都合が起きる」というパターンにおおむね当てはまるように思う。つまり、現在直面している課
題、あるいは将来直面しそうな課題に対応するには、より高い経済成長が必要という認識が成長戦略の
基礎にあると考えられる。念頭に置かれている課題の中身は、社会保障や財政の持続性、行政・医療・
介護サービスの維持、国際的な存在感の回復、生活水準の向上など、論者により様々である。パイを増
やせるなら改善できる課題は多いので、経済成長を高めるべきという主張は万人受けし、政治家には格
好の政策アジェンダになる。 
しかし、「何らかの課題を経済成長で解決する」という考え方は 、「その課題に直接向き合うより経済
成長を高める方が簡単だ」という前提がないと成立しない。その前提が間違っていれば、むしろ経済成
長に頼るがゆえに肝心の課題がいつまでも解決しないことになる。重要な課題であればあるほど、経済
成長と切り離して解決する努力を早く始めるべきではないか。 
例えば、社会保障や財政の持続性が課題なのであれば、給付と負担のバランスや、恵まれている人と
そうでない人の公平感を改善することで、問題の解決を目指す方が良い。人々の生活水準を高めたいと
いう課題を経済成長と切り離すのは少し厄介だが、国民全員が等しく困っているわけではなく、問題の

### Page 3

3

本質は経済格差にある。分配政策により、国民生活の満足感や社会の安定性を全体として改善すること
はできる。 
国際社会における日本の存在感を高めることは、経済成長ではそもそも無理であろう。かつて日本が
世界第2 位のGDP をキープし、経済大国として存在感を示した時代もあった。しかし、あれは冷戦構造
のもとでの日米関係、戦後の高度成長とその余熱、新興国が発展初期段階にあったことなどによる歴史
の偶然であった。GDP の対世界シェアが3～4％程度まで落ち、今後さらに人口が減り、資源・エネルギ
ーに乏しく、特殊な言語を使う島国が、今から経済成長を多少高められたとしても、そのことで示せる
国際パワーは高が知れている。小さくても存在感がある国をどう作るかが、正しいアジェンダ設定だと
思われる。 
21 世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」は？ 
日本が課題だらけなのは確かだが、25 年間どうやっても達成できなかった経済成長に、その解決をこ
れ以上頼るのが正解とは思いにくい。逆に、優先課題の解決に国が本気でリソースを投入するなら、そ
こに民間も収益機会を見出し、様々な連鎖反応を通じて経済に好影響が及ぶ可能性も出てくる。 
経済学者マリアナ・マッツカートは著書「ミッション・エコノミー」において、大きな変革は市場メ
カニズムだけでは生み出せず、国家が野心的だが実行可能な課題を設定し、民間の力を引き出す仕組み
を築くことが重要と説く 2。マッツカートは代表例として、1960 年代の米国アポロ計画を挙げる。1969
年 7 月 20 日、 ニール・アームストロングとバズ・オルドリンが月面に降り立ったことで知られる、あの
アポロ計画である。 
日本の成長戦略も「野心的な課題」を掲げたものではあるが、複雑すぎるマクロ経済そのものを課題
の対象にすると、先月の論考で詳しく述べたように、目的と手段の関係性が特定できない。原因と結果
の関係がよくわからない仕事は、責任の所在があいまいで互いに人任せになり、結果にコミットするこ
との効果が得られない。それに対し、「月に人を送る」などのより具体的なミッションの場合、そのため
には何が必要なのかの因果関係を分析・認識しやすい分、結果にコミットすれば人々の力が正しい方向
へ向かう。 
アポロ計画は、半導体、コンピューター、通信、材料工学などの技術を飛躍的に発展させ、その後の
米国の産業基盤を築いたと言われている。また、計画推進の国家機関であった米航空宇宙局 （NASA）は 、
多くの科学技術人材を育成・輩出した。彼らはその後、金融イノベーションなどでも活躍し、NASA 出身
なので「ロケット・サイエンティスト」と呼ばれたことはよく知られている。 
ここで重要なのは、半導体やコンピューターなどの産業育成自体を目的として、政府が補助金で支援
したという話ではないことである。アポロ計画という具体的なミッションがまず存在し、それへの貢献
に適正な対価で報いる仕組みがイノベーターたちを突き動かし、様々な技術や知見が開発、蓄積された
のである。後世に残った産業基盤や人材は、「月に人を送る」というミッションの副産物であった。

2 Mazzucato, Mariana(2021) Mission Economy: A Moonshot Guide to Changing Capitalism, Allen Lane-Penguin 
（関美和・鈴木絵里子訳『ミッション・エコノミー』News Picks、2021 年 ）。

### Page 4

4

一般論として、何らかの課題を解決すれば、経済に必ず大きなプラスの効果が見込める、とまでは言
えない。しかし、上位目標が「課題の解決」なのだから、それが達成された時点でミッションは成功で
あり、投入されたリソースは「有効に使われた」ことになる。そのうえで、マクロ経済にもプラスの波
及効果があればなお良いが、仮にそれがなくても課題が解決された分、以前より望ましい社会になって
いる。 
最大の難関は、優先課題を特定しそれに取り組む社会的モメンタムを生み出すことであり、それには
既存の発想に捉われない強力なビジョンとリーダーシップが要る。アポロ計画にも当初「無謀で財政資
金の無駄使い」という声があった。マッツカートは先ほどの著書の中で、仮に現代風の費用対効果分析
を適用していたら、アポロ計画は採用されず、人類が月面に立つことはなかったと言っている。 
アポロ計画に相当する案件は、どの時代、どの国にも自明なものとしてあるわけではない。 「月に人を
送る」というミッションは、当時の米ソ冷戦状況において、ソ連に宇宙開発で打ち勝つことが米国の死
活問題、という歴史特殊的な認識のもとで生まれた。それでも、よく考えてみれば今の日本にも、人口
の減少 ・ 高齢化や地政学環境の激変など、 人類史上にもまれな歴史特殊的な条件のもとで、 「死活問題」
になりつつある課題はいくつかあるように思う。例えば、エネルギーや食料の安定供給、生活インフラ ・
医療・介護体制の維持・改善などは、次世代の日本人にとってまさに「死活問題」なのではないか。 
限られたリソースの優先課題への配分が、市場メカニズムで実現できないなら、国が強力に関与しな
ければならない。民間には不得意な国民的課題の解決こそ、国の本来の役割であり、そのためのリソー
スを国債発行でまかないきれないなら、国民に追加負担を求めてもいい。 
言うまでもなくこれは困難な作業である。だから優先課題は何か、そのために国民負担を求めるべき
かなどを深く議論せず、経済成長で様々な問題をまとめて解決できることにしておく方が、実はずっと
楽なのである。しかし、それが問題の先送りにすぎないことは繰り返し述べたとおりである。今も成長
戦略が重要だと言い、政府にそれを求めたがるわれわれの意識は、日本が直面する真の課題やその優先
順位を考えるのは面倒だ、 という思考停止から生まれているのかもしれない。21 世紀ニッポンの「アポ
ロ計画」は何なのか、国民的な議論の活性化が必要ではないだろうか。

3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

YoY in 3Q26; Looking ahead, we expect +14% QoQ in 4Q26E, riding on new AI server 
racks ramp up and optical networking specification upgrade toward 1.6T optical

supply to better capture the demand. We keep our 2027E-28E estimates largely 
unchanged.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price from 2029E P/E discounted back 
to 2027E at 10.5% COE (no change), and derive our target P/E multiple from refreshed 
peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM). Based on our 
2029E-30E NI YoY of 18% (unchanged) and 2029-30E OPM of 38% (unchanged), and 
peers’ PEG&M of 0.4x (unchanged), we derive target P/E multiple at 22.0x (vs. 22.2x 
previously), reflecting a slight peer group multiple compression amid the product 
transition window from 800G to 1.6T optical transceivers, balanced by sustained 
long-term AI optical networking demand. Our new 12-month target price is at NT$785 
(vs. NT$793 previously). Our new TP implies 2027E P/E at 68.7x (vs. 69.4x previously), 
within the company’s trading range (vs. peak at 74x, and +1stv. at 47x). Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.

 政策解读
10 月 16 日，生态环境部对 11 项温室气体自愿减排项目方法学征求意
见。这是 CCER 市场重启以来规模最大的一批。按领域分，绿色化工
与低碳燃料 5 项（可再生能源合成氨、农林生物质气化制甲醇、农林
生物质气化耦合绿氢制甲醇、利用废弃油脂生产烃基生物柴油作为燃
料使用、利用废弃油脂生产酯基生物柴油作为燃料使用），储能 3 项
（抽水蓄能电站、钠离子电池储能电站、全钒液流电池储能电站），
交通运输 1 项（矿山运输用车电动化）、资源循环利用 2 项（低值废
塑料化学再生、厨余垃圾集中厌氧处理沼气工程）。
全国温室气体自愿减排交易市场支持领域扩大，据统计，截至 2025 年
年底，我国已发布 CCER 方法学 18 个，分别是造林碳汇、并网光热
发电、并网海上风力发电、红树林营造、公路隧道照明系统节能、甲

烷体积浓度低于 8%的煤矿低浓度瓦斯和风排瓦斯利用、海上油田伴生
气回收利用、陆上气田试气放喷气回收利用、陆上油田低气量伴生气
回收利用、淤地坝碳汇、滨海盐沼植被修复、海草床植被修、规模化
猪场粪污沼气回收利用工程、农业废弃物集中处理工程、既有公共建
筑围护结构与供暖通风空调系统能效提升、可再生能源电解水制氢、
电气设备六氟化硫回收和净化、中深层地热能井下换热供暖。
全国温室气体自愿减排交易市场登记的核证自愿减排量除用于全国碳
排放权交易市场和碳排放权交易试点市场配额清缴抵销外，还可用于
大型活动碳中和、企业环境社会治理、生态修复等多样场景。2024 年
1 月，全国温室气体自愿减排交易市场正式启动。
根据 2026 年全国碳市场发展报告数据，截至 2026 年 8 月底，全国温
室气体自愿减排交易市场累计登记自愿减排项目 41 个、核证自愿减排
量 2423.35 万吨，核证自愿减排量累计成交量 2170.68 万吨、成交额
17.37 亿元。

ESG 与可持续发展周报
2024 年启动标准前期研究，联合青海大学等科研单位，组建标准编制
组，推进标准研究制定。截至目前，依托绿电溯源成果，青海已建成
全省 992 家企业的“碳账户”，有效支撑“碳账户”贷款发放，推动
绿色金融精准服务低碳企业。
（2）“十五五”时期，全国规划建成 500 个零碳工厂，同时将非化石
能源电力消费物理认定量占比目标提升至 35%。这一政策背景让零碳
工厂成为新型储能最具确定性的应用场景之一。业内人士认为，在政
策硬约束驱动下，储能正从过去单一的峰谷调节工具，升级为支撑绿
电消纳、算电协同、碳资产管理与源网荷储一体化的关键枢纽，配套
储能设施也从工厂建设的可选项加速转变为必选项。与此同时，经济
性瓶颈、长时储能技术缺口、精细化运营能力不足，以及绿色供应链
的国际合规挑战，仍是储能全面落地工厂场景必须跨越的坎。
 技术前沿
《2026 全球碳中和年度进展报告》（以下简称《报告》）近日发布。
《报告》指出，2010—2024 年，全球光伏降本中超过六成、风电降本

中超过八成来自我国，我国风光成本下降对全球产生溢出效应。当
前，我国已形成低碳技术的全球领先优势。2025 年我国新增可再生能
源装机 440 吉瓦，约占全球的 64%，风光度电成本居全球最低区间；
纯电动车销量 820 万辆，保有量占全球的 58.9%。在新兴技术领域，
绿 氢新 增 产能 占 全球 的 70% ， 新型 储 能装 机 136 吉 瓦、 同 比增 长
84%，碳捕集、利用与封存（CCUS）运行项目 20 个、居全球第二，
五年森林生物质碳储量增加约 11.5 亿吨。
《报告》指出，全球低碳转型正进入由技术驱动、系统条件决定的新
阶段。与此同时，风光储、电动车等成熟技术加速发展，但电网消纳
能力、融资、市场机制等方面存在的不足仍制约着市场替代；清洁
氢、CCUS 等新兴技术仍面临需求、基础设施和商业模式不足的问
题。
 研究探索
10 月 20 日，在由中国金融四十人论坛（CF40）与清华大学（THU）
联合主办的 2026 外滩年会上，2026 外滩绿色金融报告（下称报告）

正式发布。本次报告主题为“绿色低碳协同发展及金融支持研究”。
中国人民银行研究局局长、CF40 成员王信担任课题负责人。报告跳出
“就绿色论绿色”的传统视角，提出“协同发展”应对多重目标、多
元风险叠加复杂格局的路径：第一层是绿色低碳发展自身的协同，包
括气候减缓与气候适应的协同、应对气候变化与自然生态保护的协
同；第二层是绿色低碳转型与国内宏观经济金融的协同；第三层是国
际视角下的协同。
报告选取农业、城市、健康医疗三个代表性领域，测算“十五五”期
间气候适应投资规模。预计“十五五”期间，农业领域气候适应投资
总需求约 1.05 万亿元，年均约 2100 亿元；39 个深化气候适应型城市
（区）建设试点地区气候适应总投资需求约 3000 亿至 4000 亿元，年
均约 600 亿至 800 亿元，扩展到全国，年均投资需求规模约 4000 亿
至 5400 亿元；健康医疗领域气候适应投资总需求约 1.88 万亿元，年
均约 3800 亿元。绿色消费方面，2025 年我国绿色消费市场规模达

12.18 万亿元，占社零总额 24.3%。报告预计，2030 年我国绿色消费
将突破 18 万亿元，占比提升至 30%左右。
王信表示，需要完善高级别政策协同机制，逐步把气候、自然等因素
纳入国民经济发展战略、财政管理框架、货币政策框架以及宏观审慎

管理框架。同时完善并落地可持续金融标准，根据试用情况完善转型
金融标准、生物多样性金融标准以及蓝色金融标准。
 国际合作
今年的联合国气候变化大会（COP31）将于 11 月 9 日至 20 日在土耳
其安塔利亚举行，围绕《联合国气候变化框架公约》及其《巴黎协
定》实施涉及的减缓、适应、资金、公正转型等近百项议题开展谈判
磋商。
生态环境部副部长李高介绍，他将再次担任中国代表团团长。生态环
境部将会同外交部等十多个部委、研究机构，以及香港、澳门特别行
政区政府，共同组成中国代表团参会。
气候变化是全球共同面临的严峻挑战。中国是《联合国气候变化框架
公约》最早的 10 个缔约方之一。
李高表示，目前全球气候治理的重心已经从规则谈判转向行动落实。
中方支持 COP31 聚焦“落实”，大会应深入讨论发达国家减排力
度、资金支持不足的“老问题”和单边主义、保护主义损害应对气候
变化集体努力的“新挑战”。

#rbnz #newzealand
First Impressions: RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026
By Kelly Eckhold & Westpac New Zealand Economics
12:30 October 28 2026 Share
The RBNZ left the OCR unchanged at 2.75% (in contrast to market expectation for a hike,
including ourselves).
The MPC has stuck to its strategy of pausing to assess the impact of tightening already done. There is
a clear split among the MPC members on where the risks lie although it seems clear enough that

higher interest rates are in the future the data evolves as the Committee expects. Key factors to focus
on are data on inflation expectations, the global economy (and potential downside risks there) and the

### Page 2

path of fuel prices.  We expect a 25 bp increase in the OCR in December to 3% and are reverting to
the OCR profile we had up until mid last week.  We don't think the December hike is a done deal as the
data will inform the next move. We will revert with more detail after the press conference. 
Key takeouts from the minutes below.

Kelly Eckhold

+64 9 348 9382

Key takeouts
Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The next policy
decision
Future policy decisions will depend
on the Committee’s judgement of the
balance of risks to medium-term
inflation.
Members discussed that information
arriving over the next six weeks
could meaningfully change the
appropriate policy stance in either
direction.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
All members agreed that tighter
monetary policy is likely to be
required, but remains conditional on
the economic outlook.
  However, the future OCR path is not
pre-determined.

The medium term
direction for the
OCR
The Committee judges that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
appropriate to return inflation to the
2 percent target mid-point while
supporting growth and employment.
Further OCR increases likely, but
are conditional on the outlook

### Page 3

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  All members agreed that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
consistent with achieving the
medium-term inflation target,
conditional on the outlook.
If domestic pricing pressures fail to
moderate sufficiently or broaden
beyond what costs and normal pass-
through can explain, this could
warrant further tightening.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
On the other hand, subdued pricing
pressures could require holding at
future meetings.
    And if the global economy turns
sharply down, materially weakening
demand and the inflation outlook,
this could alter the desired stance,
including leading to monetary
easing.

The neutral OCR   The Committee acknowledged that
the neutral interest rate has a wide
range of plausible estimates.
    Members noted that the increase in
global long-term yields may partly
signal a higher global neutral
interest rate, due to higher inflation
expectations, war destruction and
demand for capital to support AI
investment.
    Higher global interest rates could
bring forward a potential global
downturn, and the neutral interest
rate could decline rapidly in the
event of an abrupt global
adjustment.

### Page 4

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The strength of
economic activity
After lacklustre growth in the June
quarter, New Zealand's economic
recovery has most likely resumed
but remains uneven.
The New Zealand economy
continues to recover, although
conditions remain uneven.
  The recovery is expected to
strengthen and broaden.
The Committee noted that GDP
growth was more than expected
through the first half of 2026.
  The Committee assessed that
growth was lacklustre in the second
quarter of 2026.
Business survey measures point to
continued expansion in economic
activity.
  The economic recovery is expected
to have resumed in the third quarter,
while remaining uneven across
sectors and regions.
Household consumption has been
weak and the aggregate saving rate
is estimated to be 3.7 percent,
above the 10-year average of 1
percent.

The global
economic
outlook
The global economy is facing
significant risks that could affect
commodity prices and demand for
exports.
Global growth has been resilient but
is increasingly fragile given
geopolitical tensions, high energy
prices, financial stresses, and
surging AI-related investment.
  Despite recent geopolitical events,
economic growth in New Zealand’s
trading partners has remained
resilient and the outlook has
improved.
The Committee assessed that there
are significant risks to the global
economic outlook.

### Page 5

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  Although risks to the global economy
remain heightened, the Committee
expects global economic conditions
to remain favourable for New
Zealand’s export facing businesses
over the medium term.
The Committee noted that a global
downturn could occur abruptly.

The labour
market
Conditions in the labour market
should improve as the recovery
gathers pace.
Employment growth remains
subdued and higher prices will
further reduce real incomes and
purchasing power.
  Employment growth has not been
sufficient to fully absorb new
entrants into the labour market and
unemployment is elevated,
particularly in Auckland and
Wellington and for youth and the
long-term unemployed.
Employment intentions remain
weak, pointing to continued slow
recovery in the labour market.
  The labour market is expected to
slowly improve, with employment
growing and unemployment falling
as the economy recovers.
To date, wage growth has been
subdued, and medium-term inflation
expectations have remained stable.
  On balance, the Committee
assesses that spare capacity
remains in the economy, particularly
in the labour market.

### Page 6

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The short term
inflation outlook
Inflation increased to 4.1 percent in
the June quarter because of higher
fuel prices arising from the conflict in
the Middle East.
Annual inflation is expected to
increase to 4.4 percent in the
December quarter, largely reflecting
current futures prices for fuel.
  The Committee expects inflation to
remain elevated this year before
returning to the target band by mid-
2027 and reaching the 2 percent
mid-point later next year.
Near-term inflation forecasts are
uncertain given volatility in prices for
oil and refined fuels.
    Direct effects of higher fuel prices
will drop out of the annual inflation
calculation in late 2027 if prices for
refined petroleum products stabilise
or fall.

The medium term
inflation outlook
Core inflation, expected wage
growth, and inflation expectations
remain consistent with inflation
returning to the 1 to 3 percent target
band by mid-2027 and the 2 percent
target midpoint later next year.
The Committee is focused on
returning inflation to the 2 percent
target mid-point over the medium
term, discounting temporary
disturbances to inflation and
avoiding unnecessary economic
instability.
  Longer-term inflation expectations
remain near 2 percent, while most
measures of one and two-year-
ahead inflation expectations have
fallen since May.
Impacts on shortterm pricing
behaviour appear to have been
contained and medium-term inflation
expectations remain well anchored.
  Currently, indicators of medium-term
inflation are consistent with inflation
returning to target.
But the longer inflation remains high,
the greater the risk of higher costs
feeding into inflation expectations
and price- and wage-setting
behaviour, making inflation more
persistent.

### Page 7

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The balance of
risks for
economic growth
and inflation
New Zealand’s economic recovery
could be stronger or weaker than
expected and price pressures could
generate more persistent inflation.
New Zealand’s recovery could be
stronger than expected but could
also lose momentum if the global
environment deteriorates or export
strength does not flow through to
jobs, incomes and household
spending.
  Consumption growth could be
subdued if employment and house
price growth remain weak, or if
precautionary behaviour persists.
There is a risk that inflation becomes
entrenched before conventional
expectations measures respond,
despite the previous two OCR
increases.
  Alternatively, consumption growth
could be stronger and absorb spare
capacity faster than currently
anticipated.
In addition, the world economy could
tip abruptly into a downturn causing
weaker inflation over the medium
term.
  All members agreed that downside
risks to activity were significant and
that the recovery could remain
uneven.
It is also possible that geopolitical
escalation could drive fuel prices
higher while weakening global
economic activity at the same time.

Key arguments
advanced by
more hawkish
MPC members
Hayley Gourley, Karen Silk,
Prasanna Gai and Anna Breman
saw upside risks to inflation relative
to the central projection.
Hayley Gourley, Paul Conway and
Karen Silk favoured increasing the
OCR by 25 basis points at this
meeting.

### Page 8

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  These members observed that more
persistence in energy and
petrochemical prices could raise
near-term inflation risks, impact
price-setting behaviour, and lead to
more sticky inflation over the
medium term.
These members emphasised that
core inflation has been above levels
consistent with headline inflation at
the target mid-point for some time.
  Price-setting can be a front-loaded
process, meaning businesses could
raise domestic prices more than
import costs warrant.
This increases the risk of inflation
becoming entrenched in price-
setting behaviour as the conflict and
its consequences endure, including
through higher freight and shipping
costs.
  These factors increase the risk that
monetary policy needs to lean
against broader inflation pressures.
Acting now would guard against
sustained inflation pressures and
potentially reduce the risk of needing
to do more with interest rates later.

Key arguments
advanced by
more dovish MPC
members in each
meeting
Paul Conway and Carl Hansen saw
risks to inflation as balanced.
Anna Breman, Carl Hansen,
Rebecca Williams and Prasanna Gai
favoured holding the OCR at this
meeting as two recent OCR
increases allow the Committee to
take further time to assess their
effects and economic developments.
  These members shared concerns
that high inflation could become
embedded through price-setting
behaviour.
Actual inflation was lower than
expected, reflecting smaller indirect
effects of higher fuels prices, and
medium-term inflation expectations
and wage growth remain consistent
with returning inflation to the target
mid-point.

activity risks as skewed to the

in household consumption and

They highlighted the potential effects

They favoured pausing at this

Great improvement in 3Q26

Intel reports 3Q26 sales of US$16.5bn, 2% qoq and 21% yoy, in line
with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much
higher than Bloomberg’s US$0.28 largely due to 2ppts better gross
margin, 4ppts better operating margin and non-operating income of
US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from
3Q25’s 30%. Operating margin for data center IC sales was 42%,
much higher than Bloomberg’s 27%, and improved from 2Q26’s
40% and 3Q25’s 23%.
Improve in progress for foundry business: In addition to
stronger data center IC sales, Intel foundry 3Q26 sales improved to
US$6.7bn, up 16% qoq and 58% yoy. Driven by higher utilization
rate, 3Q26 operating margin for Intel foundry was improved to -28%

### Page 2

from 2Q26’s -36% and 3Q25’s -55%, which was much better than
Bloomberg’s -36% estimates.
Source: Intel, Bloomberg est.

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS
Combined eﬀects from lack of demand on client computing (due to
memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage
inventory might extend to 1H27) and lack of data center CPU
capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

down aﬀects 2ppts sales). 4Q26 gross margin is guided to
deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).

Document 404: Regional Roundup_ Coast-to-coast growth From spending to wages, growth is broad-based across the US, though the South is the clear leader in new business applications. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Regional Roundup_ Coast-to-coast growth From spending to wages, growth is broad-based across the US, though the South is the clear leader in new business applications. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

13035502     1

Economy 
Regional Roundup: Coast-to-coast growth 
29 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per household is growing at healthy rates across the country. 
Discretionary spending growth has improved markedly relative to the last two years. While the West has led recently and the 
South has lagged somewhat, no region appears to be experiencing a meaningful consumer slowdown. 
• The South has seen softer discretionary retail spending, particularly in home-related durable goods categories. However, the 
region continues to post the strongest after-tax wage growth and has been the largest contributor to growth in new business 
applications nationwide. 
• Business formation is occurring across a wide range of industries, with professional services, retail, healthcare, construction and 
hospitality all accounting for meaningful shares of new business applications. While entrepreneurial activity is broad-based 
across industries nationwide, industry revenue mixes vary considerably by region.

Spending growth is solid across all regions  
Spending growth was strongest in the West in September, while spending growth lagged in the Northeast and South, though not 
by much. Total credit and debit card spending per household increased around 5.6% year-over-year (YoY) in the West, outpacing 
other regions by less than a percentage point, according to Bank of America aggregated card data (Exhibit 1). All regions also 
experienced major improvements in spending growth compared to last year, increasing by over three percentage points in every 
region.   
Spending growth in the Northeast peaked at around 6% YoY in June but remained a solid 4.8% YoY in September. The slowest 
growth was in the South, though spending was still up nearly 4.8% YoY.

spending was also up around 4.8% YoY in both regions, still a very healthy rate of growth.

Durables spending is weighing on discretionary retail spending in the South 
One of the key themes from September in the overall spending data was a broadening in spending growth, particularly in “more 
discretionary” retail categories like clothing, electronics, building materials and non-store/miscellaneous retailers (read more in 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers). Bank of America card data shows that this improvement is occurring across all 
regions.  
However, the South lags, though this is mostly due to declines in spending on home-related durables in, for instance, home 
improvement stores and at furniture retailers. In our view, this could reflect some slowdown in domestic migration to the Sun 
Belt and a corresponding easing in home/moving-related spending (read more in On the move: Renovation over relocation?)

The South leads in after-tax wage and business application growth 
One reason we are not overly concerned about consumer spending in the South is the strength of after-tax wage growth in the 
region. Bank of America deposit data suggests that after-tax wage growth was strongest there, though only by a slim margin, up 
nearly 4.2% YoY, just ahead of households in the Midwest, and outpacing the West and Northeast by less than half a percentage 
point (Exhibit 4).

Beyond wage income, business ownership offers another potential channel for wealth accumulation. The South has been the 
primary contributor to overall US growth in new business applications so far in 2026, according to US Census Bureau data 
(Exhibit 5).  
While historically only a small share of these applications is likely to become a business employing workers, this surge in 
applications points to a potential pipeline of entrepreneurial activity. For businesses that become established and profitable, 
ownership can provide a pathway to wealth through retained earnings and the accumulation of business equity. Still, the extent 
of that opportunity depends on businesses’ survival, profitability and financing needs (read more in Business Checkpoint: Looking 
into logistics).

Business application growth is widespread across regions and industries 
Looking at 2026 business applications by industry, entrepreneurial activity spans multiple industry groupings (Exhibit 6). 
Professional, scientific, technical and other services account for the largest share, followed by retail trade and the grouping 
comprising healthcare, education services and the arts. Additionally, businesses provide a significant source of income for US 
households. In fact, business revenues accounted for nearly 14% of total pre-tax income, according to the US Census’ 2025 
Survey of Consumer Finances.

Combined, this suggests that potential pathways to business ownership extend not only across various households (read more on 
generational shifts in entrepreneurship in July’s A new generation of business formation) but also across many industries, rather 
than a single sector. 
While business applications show the breadth of prospective entrepreneurial activity nationwide, the industry composition of 
existing business revenue offers a regional lens on business activity. Exhibit 7 shows how revenue was distributed across 
industries within regions according to the 2024 Annual Integrated Economic Survey from the US Census Bureau, highlighting the 
extent to which regional economies are concentrated in particular sectors. 
Although wholesale trade was the sector with the largest share of revenue in each region in the US, there is notable variance in 
economic activity from business revenues across the country. For example, finance and insurance accounted for more than 20% 
of revenue in the Northeast, whereas it’s about half that in the West. Also, manufacturing was only 8.5% of revenue in the 
Northeast, and more than 20% in the Midwest. Overall, the data shows a dynamic national business landscape, with broad 
entrepreneurial activity but distinct regional differences in the industries generating revenue.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend includes total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs. Value grocers is defined using a combination of MCC code and proprietary methods.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

The oil market is likely to remain nervous this week ahead of
the US midterm elections. The market has been increasingly
pricing in the risk of escalation in the Persian Gulf following the
elections, but there is still a glimmer of hope for diplomacy

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – Ukraine resume attacks on Russian
reﬁneries
Oil prices ﬁnished strong last week, with ICE Brent settling almost
2.4% higher on Friday to close above $113/bbl. It was also the Dec-
26 contract expiry; it settled at $117.64/bbl on its last trading day.
Vessels have faced a growing number of attacks in recent weeks.
Yet this coincides with signs that more traﬃc is ﬂowing through the
Strait of Hormuz. Fears remain high over the potential for fresh US
and Israeli strikes on Iran following the US midterms. Reports say
that President Trump wants to give diplomacy a chance are
weighing on prices in early morning trading today. Iranian
negotiators have been in Doha over the weekend, holding talks with

Qatari mediators. It remains to be seen whether this will lead to
anything.
The price action in oil in recent weeks reﬂects a market increasingly
bracing for the potential for more hostilities between the US and
Iran. If the midterms pass with neither strikes nor a deal, the key
question is whether the market continues to price in a substantial
geopolitical risk premium or whether that premium gradually
begins to unwind. Given that many of the key developments in this

conﬂict have unfolded over weekends, the market may have to
hold its breath until the end of the week.
Meanwhile, in Russia, the brief respite that reﬁners received from
Ukraine appears to have ended. Attacks on Russian reﬁners
resumed towards the end of last week. The roughly 2-week pause
had seen the ICE gasoil crack fall below $70/bbl and trade to its
lowest level since early October. Further attacks would likely provide
a ﬂoor to the crack, with strikes casting doubt over how much diesel

Russia will export. Russia’s deputy prime minister said that Russia
plans to export 2.15m tonnes of diesel in November, up from a little
more than 500kt in October.
OPEC+ met over the weekend to discuss output policy for
December, with no surprises. Members decided to keep output
targets unchanged for the month. Given disruptions in the Persian
Gulf, most members are producing well below their production
target.
The latest positioning data shows that speculators increased their

net long in ICE Brent by 3,576 lots over the last reporting week to
245,457 lots as of last Tuesday. Fresh longs drove the move.
Meanwhile, the net speculative long is still some distance away
from the year-to-date high of around 430k lots seen in March. This
suggests that with the right catalyst, speculators could add to this
position. A strong catalyst would be needed, though, given that
from a risk-reward perspective it may not make sense to take too

much risk on the long side at current price levels.

EUR104/MWh. Prices eased slightly in early morning trading today.
Weather forecasts for NW Europe show colder-than-usual weather
this week, leading to potentially large storage draws. EU gas
storage peaked at just under 75% last Tuesday and has been
edging lower since, suggesting the heating season is now
underway. With a tight balance through the winter, the market is
likely to remain extremely volatile, and weather will prove to be
even more important than usual.

Metals - Gold hits one-year low
Gold fell to its lowest level in a year at the end of last week as oil
prices surged amid inﬂation concerns and expectations that
interest rates could stay higher for longer. Prices dropped 3.65% to
$3,874.62/oz, with the sell-oﬀ in technology shares adding to
pressure as investors reduced positions to cover losses elsewhere.
For now, higher energy prices, rate expectations and the risk of
further liquidation across broader markets remain headwinds for

precious metals.
Silver has been hit harder, sliding more than 6% and extending its
October decline to roughly 18%. The widening gold-silver ratio also
points to gold continuing to outperform silver during the latest
precious-metals correction.
In industrial metals, copper prices also moved lower as the wider
risk-oﬀ mood spilled into markets. Underlying fundamentals
remain supportive, with signs of tightness still evident across the
physical market. The LME cash-to-three-month backwardation

narrowed by almost $48/t on Friday to $174.85/t, but remains
elevated, while the Yangshan premium rose above $200/t last
week. This suggests reﬁned copper availability remains constrained
despite the pullback in futures prices. That tightness should provide
a ﬂoor under prices, although at elevated levels we could see some
resistance from price-sensitive demand.

pressure

CBOT corn prices extended losses for a second consecutive month,

June 2023. The sell-oﬀ was driven by increased harvest supplies,
weaker export demand, and easing concerns over Black Sea
disruptions. US farmers had harvested 60% of their corn crop as of
25 October, up from 44% a week earlier, boosting near-term supply.
Weekly US corn export sales declined 15% week-on-week to
1,303.9kt as of 22 October.

Market sentiment is under pressure amid expectations of lower
geopolitical risk following renewed discussions involving Russia,
Ukraine, and the US. This could mean more stable grain shipments
from the Black Sea region. Speculators reduced their net long corn
positions by 90,314 lots for the sixth consecutive week to 55,314
lots. Traders took proﬁts following the summer weather-driven rally
and reassessed supply risks amid improving crop conditions and

easing geopolitical tensions.
Among other soft crops, CFTC data shows that money managers
increased their net bearish bets in CBOT wheat by 23,725 lots to
74,859 lots as of 27 October 2026. The move was driven by a rise in
gross short positions. This was driven by improving harvest
progress in Australia and Argentina, coupled with expectations for
the second-largest global wheat crop. For soybeans, the net
speculative long position fell by 21,536 lots to 185,400 lots, as

stronger export competitiveness from Brazil prompted traders to
reduce bullish exposure.

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2
nd 
Novem
ber 2026

### Page 2

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.6% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of German defence spending on the real economy . Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we anticipate another 
25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That said, strong er than 
expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q  in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aero nautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before  rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.
